    “I feel like I should steal this little glass,” Kara says. She looks as though she’s in despair over the peril her mortal soul’s been placed in by the temptation of the half-pint of Guinness she’s managed to order. Lena has no such concerns.

    “Finish it and I’ll put it in my purse,” Lena says. Kara giggles about it. It’s Friday night in Dublin, and the pubs are bustling in the comfortable warmth that Lena’s heard several passerby call hot. They’re loitering outside their fourth stop of the night after a nice dinner with nice cocktails, which means Kara is in her suit and looks delectable.

    “Ooh, customs is gonna get you,” Kara says, pointing at Lena with the hand she has her tiny little Guinness in. “I’ll rescue you though, babe.”

    “My hero,” Lena says, sipping her own full Guinness. Her other hand is, mysteriously, finding the gap between buttons on Kara’s nice shirt. “When’s the car coming?”

    “When we call it,” Kara says. Lena squints at her.

    “Didn’t you call it when we left the last place?” Lena asks. Kara scrambles, dislodging Lena’s hand and plunging her hand into her suit pocket to find her phone. Lena watches with amusement as she nearly blinds herself with the phone’s light. 

    “Oh, shit, I did,” Kara says. “Oh, but they gave me an ETA of…one seventeen.”

    Kara holds her phone screen out with a grin, to show Lena that it is, in fact, twelve forty nine. 

    “Imma set an alarm,” Kara mutters, setting her baby Guinness on the window sill next to them so she can use both hands on her phone. Lena takes the opportunity to put her hand back where it was, feeling Kara’s abs under the soft fabric of her shirt. 

    “Hey! Hey, you’re Lena Luthor!” a man’s voice shouts. Lena freezes in place, and Kara looks up immediately, but they both find themselves looking at a regular man in a jacket and with a Titleist hat jammed on his head. He’s with a pack of friends of mixed gender, and they all sort of stumble all over each other as they come to a halt to stare at her. She stares back. Kara makes a noise like she’s about to talk -

    “Yes! Erin go braugh!” someone yells amongst the pack. “Good luck at The Open! We’re with you!”

    Lena is a bit shellshocked, but waves at them, and says, “Thank you.”

    “Your brother’s a poxy bastard!” someone else yells. Lena laughs abruptly as the whole group erupts in raucous, surely very drunk cheers. Lena is only just barely drunk, so she holds herself from joining in. She thinks that’s the end of it, truthfully, but she and Kara watch as they start jumping up and down and someone’s chanting Lena again, ole ole like they’re at a soccer match.

    “Oh my God, I have to - ” Kara mumbles, fumbling with her phone again as she clearly rushes to record this…strangely wholesome event. 

    The group goes on with the chant for a few rounds before they disperse without another word - aside from one of them turning back to Lena and yelling kick all their arses! - which prompts them into another round as they continue down the street. Several other people hanging around outside their pub or other pubs look on with amusement, but Lena catches a few of them clapping along.

    “Babe, I think you might be a rock star,” Kara says, reaching forward to grab for Lena’s hip while Lena is still watching them disappear around a corner. She’s in heels on a decidedly old cobblestone street, so she mostly stumbles into Kara when she tugs at her. “Maybe we should move here.”

    “Are two and a half houses not enough for you?” Lena asks, raising an eyebrow and withdrawing just enough to sip her Guinness. Kara scoffs.

    “One of them is in Florida,” Kara says with open derision. Lena is forced to allow that.

    “Lillian always said investing in property overseas was only acceptable if it were in a tax haven,” Lena says. Kara groans, reaching for her own baby Guinness and taking an enormous gulp of it. While the tasting guy at the Guinness museum had said that it shouldn’t be sipped, Lena’s pretty sure he didn’t also mean that it should be guzzled. But who knows. Lena had been more focused on how intently Kara had been paying attention to what the man was saying to really care.

    “Lillian is the devil, confirmed,” Kara says. She’s got a cute little foam mustache now. “Dara said so.”

    “I don’t think we needed Dara’s confirmation on that one,” Lena says, laughing and reaching up to wipe at the foam. Kara nearly falls over while trying to focus on Lena’s thumb brushing her lip, but her amusement is swiftly ended when Kara catches her hand before she pulls it away and sucks Lena’s thumb into her mouth. It’s a good move.

    “You know,” Kara says, after Lena has pulled her hand away and surreptitiously shook out the tingles at her side. “The car ride is gonna take like, thirty minutes to get back to Portmarnock.”

    Lena considers this, looking at Kara with narrowed eyes. 

    Finally she asks: “Is there a privacy screen?”

    And Kara grins like she’s caught the canary.

    PORTRUSH, NORTHERN IRELAND - DUNLUCE LINKS AT ROYAL PORTRUSH GOLF CLUB - THE OPEN CHAMPIONSHIP

     

    “So…how was Portmarnock?” Sam asks. It’s Monday night. Lena looks at her in the bathroom mirror, her lipstick hovering. Sam looks back. She’s flown in for the week, along with Jack and the usual crew. Kara and Nia are having some sort of dinner pre-game catch up that Lena had been explicitly not invited to.

    “It was good,” Lena says, eyeing Sam. She looks especially nefarious, for some reason. Maybe it’s the lighting of her hotel room. 

    “Any news?” Sam asks. Lena rolls her eyes, capping her lipstick and turning to look at Sam with a glare. 

    “You sound like your daughter,” Lena says. Sam gasps, affronted, reaching out to whap Lena with her clutch. Lena brushes past her and into her and Kara’s hotel room, rifling for the black and rose gold watch Hublot had sent for the weekend’s fancier events. The one she’s meant to wear all week is still sitting in its box, because Kara had insisted it should be a surprise. 

    “How dare you say something like that to me?” Sam asks. “I wake up at eight in the morning to fourteen texts from your assistant about how you’ve disappeared in the night to Ireland with your beau - ”

    “My beau? Really, Samantha?” Lena asks, laughing as she straps the watch in place and checks the mirror. 

    “So, to be clear, you are not engaged,” Sam says. Lena turns to look at her with such an expression that Sam takes it as an emphatic no. “Well, cool. Why’d you guys come out here early?”

    Lena stops for a moment, thinking of the photo of her mother with Dara, Aileen, and Oscar that she’d slipped between the pages of one of Kara’s books before they’d left for Portrush. When she moves over to the desk where Kara’s backpack is sitting on the chair, she has to unearth several things to get to it.

    “Okay, wow, wow, I did not need to see whatever - oh, okay,” Sam says, as Lena pulls it free from its hiding place and hands it over. Sam is focused on various items Lena’s dumped onto the desk, but turns her head to look down. Lena nearly laughs at the way her eyes widen and her mouth drops open. “Oh my God.”

    “Yeah,” Lena says. Sam looks at the photo, then Lena, and then back down to the photo, and then when she looks up again, there are obvious tears in her eyes. Lena has no warning before Sam is wrapping her up in a tight hug that she finds herself returning with a laugh that chokes off in her throat. 

    “Holy shit,” Sam whispers. “Is that your fucking mom?”

    “Yeah,” Lena whispers back. Sam laughs, pulling back a little and gripping at Lena’s cheeks. She’s crying; they’re both crying, which is a little embarrassing, but - Sam’s her best friend. She loves her. Lena’s not had many of those in her life, but she’s pretty sure you’re allowed to cry in private with your best friend. 

    “So, you found her? The - I mean, I guess, where she’s buried? And you found stuff about her?” Sam asks, pulling back a little bit more to pull the photo between them, one hand still resting on Lena’s shoulder.

    “Kara found her, really. This tiny village named Balrothery. This was her usual golf foursome. We ran into them in one of the two pubs in town,” Lena says. 

    “Wow,” Sam says. “She looks so much like you. Or - you look so much like her.”

    “Yeah,” Lena agrees. Sam stares at the photo a bit more before she looks up at Lena with a smile and somewhat teary eyes that don’t exactly help Lena compose herself.

    “We’re gonna have to redo our makeup,” Sam laughs, and then, she pulls Lena into another hug.

    -

    “Golf Channel wants you for a sitdown after your round,” Nia says, flipping through her notebook. Lena is about to head out for her first round at Royal Portrush, this year’s home of the Open. Kara is massaging her hand while she and Brainy discuss yardages. She has one of the latest tee times available, and so the clubhouse is quieter - but it still feels like everyone is watching her. There’s a miserably persistent heavy drizzle outside. 

    “Don’t I have something else after the round?” Lena asks.

    “A press conference,” Nia says. “And then ESPN. And then - ”

    “Can I do Golf Channel tomorrow?” Lena asks idly, watching as someone - she can’t really tell through the haze outside and she hadn’t contemplated the full tee sheet - hits their driver on the first tee box. Lena knows it’s windy, but the steady push the ball takes to the right is dramatic. Not a good sign. Inclement conditions had never been one of her strengths, but she feels mysteriously okay. The course had been fun over her practice rounds. 

    “I’ll ask,” Nia says, scribbling something. She’s about to say something else, but she promptly snaps her mouth shut when she sees something over Lena’s shoulder. “Hi.”

    “Hi,” a quiet voice says. It rings as familiar, and Lena turns to look only to find Eve Tessmacher standing there, looking…timid. Though Lena’s been on tour with her for a few years, they haven’t had a ton of occasion to speak, and she doubts Lex or Lillian would condone the practice in either direction. “Uh, Lena, could I talk to you for a second in private?”

    There’s a brief moment of silence as Lena’s whole table looks up at Eve. Kara’s grip on Lena’s hand is warm and strong, and on Lena’s other side, Sam has put down her phone and crossed her arms. If this were a few weeks ago, Lena might be a little more reticent, but Eve is standing here alone.

    “Sure,” Lena says, climbing up out of her chair.

    Eve leads them around the corner, toward a small room that holds different trophies that have been presented at the course. It’s quiet, but they draw eyes the whole way. There’s a strong, heavy silence for a moment. Eve looks out a window with a strange expression on her face.

    “Is something wrong?” Lena finally asks. 

    “I wanted to tell you that I’ve left Luthor Golf,” Eve says, suddenly, as though she hadn’t heard Lena speak. “And that I hate your brother. And your mother.”

    Lena is so shocked by that statement that she laughs, loudly, and Eve looks at her with wide doe eyes for a moment before she laughs too. It’s kind of nice. 

    “Are you okay, then?” Lena asks. “I know they’re a bit…”

    “Vindictive? Yes,” Eve scoffs, making Lena laugh again. She’s never seen the girl display so much personality. Though she’s not had much occasion. “Well, they obviously took my caddie, and somehow my Whoop sponsorship has disappeared, but - I’m not sure they really ever cared that much about me.”

    Lena gets the implication therein. Eve shrugs.

    “But I just wanted to tell you, because I - I guess I care about your opinion of me,” Eve says. “I only joined up with them because of you, but I have subsequently learned that your skills are definitely not because of them, because they’re the worst people. The worst. And also that your brother was the one who leaked that video of you hitting his Maserati, and he and your mother also started that rumor about you being a love child or whatever, I overheard them talking about it. I mean…it also kind of sounded like it was true, so, um. If you didn’t know that, I would maybe look into that. And I wanted to tell you too, that they’re here at this tournament, both of them, probably haunting both of us now.”

    Lena blinks. Eve takes in a deep breath after her impressive stream of consciousness.

    “Thank you for telling me,” Lena says, eventually. “And I’m sorry that they’ve hurt you, too. Would you want to…contribute to an article about how much they suck?”

    The wide, bright smile on Eve’s face is probably answer enough.

    -

    “This is terrible,” Eve says, Saturday afternoon. They’ve been grouped together for the first time since the US Open, and the crowd following them is excessively large. From what Lena can see through the deluge. 

    “Brutal,” Jack agrees, slicking his hair back before replacing his hat. Lena had let Eve know Jack was in town if she wanted a caddie she actually knew well after their conversation. Next to her, Kara is fiddling with the arrangement of gloves she’s been making in the wires of their umbrella while trying to keep Lena’s bag under it as well. 

    “I don’t even know where to aim with the weather like this,” Lena says. 

    “I’m just going to wing it,” Eve says, rather cheerfully. She’s an excessively exuberant person, as it turns out. “I went left last round but I can’t keep track of the wind today.”

    “The wind is swirling pretty badly, according to Brainy,” Kara says. Lena sighs. Her wrist is hurting from multiple trips into the thick heather grass lining the fairways, though at least everyone else is having their own misadventures. She’s maintaining a solid three shot lead this round on Eve, but it’s taking a lot more bogeys than she’s seen all year.

    “I am going to be contacting Adidas about the quality of their rainwear after this,” Jack says. Lena and Kara are in Nike and not fairing much better - Lena’s rainproof jacket had given up the ghost somewhere on the third hole and she’s been shivering like a drowned rat ever since. The heavy drizzle has remained since Thursday and is projected to continue into tomorrow. 

    “Miss Luthor, Miss Tessmacher, the group ahead has cleared the green,” the attendant at the teebox says. Jack yanks Eve’s driver out of her bag, wiping at the grip before handing it over. While she walks on up to the tee, Kara starts removing one of the dozen-or-so gloves hanging from their umbrella like a chandelier to hand over. 

    Despite Kara’s very best efforts, it’s still damp as Lena slides it on her hand and flexes.

    “So, I was thinking,” Kara whispers. Lena glares at her. They’re largely obscured by their umbrella and the unrelenting dampness but anyone with a skill for lip reading would be getting a thrilling look into their sex life with a sentence starter like that. Kara pouts. Lena reaches up to adjust her ponytail. “You don’t know what I was going to say.”

    “And I don’t want to know right now,” Lena says. “Save it for our hotel room.”

    “But this is kind of our thing, you know, I say something so sexy and then you’re distracted from your anxieties…” Kara whispers back. Eve smacks her drive, and the wind takes it to an non-ideal leftward position on the fairway. Lena finishes adjusting her ponytail, holding out her hand.

    “Driver,” Lena says. Kara looks at her for a moment, before she smiles.

    “You’re so hot,” Kara says. Lena rolls her eyes, flexing her fingers in a gesture of give me my club right now. “Like, so hot. Is this confident Lena Luthor? I think I’m in love with her.”

    “Kara, give me my club,” Lena says, laughing. Kara does manage to withdraw Lena’s driver then, wiping at the grip with her towel. Lena takes it and walks up to the tee, squinting through the rain. She’s been trying to hit her shots as low as possible to avoid the wind, and so she lines up with the ball further back than she usually would. She gives two practice swings, then steps up to the ball and swings true.

    -

    Lena wins the Open on a Sunday in Northern Ireland, completing a single season majors sweep. The biggest enemy she has on the course turns out to be the rain.

    “We’ve got to go to the thing with the thing,” Kara mutters, right up against Lena’s lips. They’re mashed together, in a small restroom in the clubhouse. Lena has her hands on Kara’s abs, damp with rain. 

    “Nia will come get us,” Lena whispers back. Kara hums and sucks on Lena’s lip. They’re meant to be quote “freshening up” for the podium ceremony at this moment in time. Kara had made some joke about getting fresh and Lena’s adrenaline had agreed vehemently. 

    “God, I want to eat you out right now,” Kara says, apparently having forgotten what her protests were. Lena smiles and kisses her again, sliding her hands up the ladder of Kara’s ribs and edging a thumb underneath the band of her bra. Before Lena can cook up a response to that, there’s a knock on the door, followed by Nia’s voice: sorry you freaks, but you have two minutes to get on the podium or NBC is gonna kill everyone.

    Kara groans. Lena laughs.

    She’s still fixing her pants as they come out, making sure her polo isn’t twisted where it’s tucked in. Nia looks both impressed and unimpressed somehow, handing over a random wet wipe to Kara to fix a smear of Lena’s lipstick on her face. 

    “So what are we supposed to do?” Kara asks as she’s rubbing vigorously at her face. Nia sighs.

    “I told you what you had to do when we walked into the clubhouse,” Nia says.

    “Did I seem like I was paying attention, Nia? God,” Kara says, shoving at Nia, who shoves right back. Lena smiles. She at least sort of remembers - something about Lena walking up all these stairs, receiving the trophy, giving an interview, Kara gets a trophy, blah blah -

    “Lena.”

    All three of them stop dead. Lex is standing in front of them in a Luthor Golf pullover and with his excessively bald head shiny with rain. Lena’s been here all week and hasn’t seen hide nor hair of either Lex or her mother despite several questions about Eve leaving their stable and the Maserati they’ve been driving all week.

    “Lex,” Lena says, stepping forward and taking point ahead of Kara and Nia. 

    “I wanted to congratulate you,” Lex says. His hands are in his pockets. He looks as casual as can be. Calm and confident. Unearned confidence, as it turns out. Lena’s whole life, she had been jealous of this man - but maybe all this time he’d been jealous of her. Better at golf, important enough to their aloof father that he’d disappear every summer to go see her when she was young and eventually brought her back with him. Better hair. 

    “Did you?” Lena asks, crossing her arms. 

    “Yes. Both of you, in fact. Kara, I think we got off on the wrong foot,” Lex says. Kara scoffs behind her, but thankfully does not sucker punch Lex in the face this time. Lena sighs.

    “Is there a point to this, Lex?” Lena asks.

    Lex looks at her for a long moment, his eyes flinty. It looks so much like Lillian that she almost says something, knowing he’d find it unconscionable. But she doesn’t feel very jocular. And apparently NBC is going to kill everyone soon. Whatever he sees, he doesn’t give away.

    “Mother and I were wrong to treat you so badly when you were clearly struggling,” Lex says. “We were talking about it recently, and we wanted to offer you a place in Luthor Golf, with a more substantive ownership stake in the company itself. Both of you, to be clear. And I should say - I miss being a part of your team, Lena. I can’t speak for mother, but - I do.”

    For one small moment, Lena feels the familiar warmth of the good kind of her brother’s attention. It had been so rare sometimes that even a moment of kindness had been enough to keep going. And this is…kind, on its face. Lena doubts that it is particularly kind anywhere underneath the surface, and it’s made more doubtable by the fact that she’s about to go accept her fourth major trophy in one season and is surely in for a round of sponsorship renegotiations and, if Sam has her way, television commercials. It’s made more doubtable by the range of cold silence to manipulative bullshit he and Lillian have both dished out all this year. 

    The moment passes by like a leaf on the breeze.

    “Lex,” Lena starts, uncrossing her arms and smiling. “Go fuck yourself.”

    She has the pleasure of watching his face freeze in place, a vein on his head popping prominently. 

    And then she’s moving, Nia and Kara giggling with her as they start down the hall and out the clubhouse doors. There are a few lightbulb flashes, and then Nia is ushering them into the huge grandstand structure set up around the 18th hole teebox, and an NBC employee with a bib on is directing she and Kara toward a stairwell made up of rickety steel scaffolding.

    “I’m going to marry the absolute shit out of you,” Kara says at the first landing as they start jogging up the steps. The whole thing sways as they go, and the only lighting is some spare work lights affixed to the railings. It’s very unglamorous, especially for someone who’s just won fifteen million dollars swinging a stick around and is wearing a Hublot watch riddled with black diamonds that’s apparently worth fifty thousand dollars. Lillian Luthor would surely sneer at the surroundings, not to mention the way she and Kara are careening up the steps. “I’m serious. Just so you know, I’m really, really serious.”

    “Don’t let Nia hear you say that, she’ll pass out. Good job not punching him, by the way,” Lena says at the next landing, brushing past Kara and taking the lead up the next set of steps. Honestly, how tall was this thing anyway? She can hear the crowd’s sounds permeating the black fabric hanging all around them. 

    “Thank you for noticing my restraint, babe,” Kara says, laughing. 

    “What a fucking asshole,” Lena says. She can see a dim light up on the next landing above them, and she swings her way around the railing to start up them. 

    “Maybe he just wants a new Maserati,” Kara says, just as they crest onto the final landing. There’s a harried looking NBC employee waiting for them looking on the edge of tears, a huge headset clamped to his ears. 

    “They’re here,” he says, hitting some button on his hip pack. Immediately, the PA stops cranking the music and an announcer starts talking. “Hey, congratulations, Miss Luthor.”

    “Thanks,” Lena says, nodding his way. He physically blushes, which is kind of cute. When she turns to look at Kara, she’s looking at Lena with a grin on her face. That’s way, way cuter. Lena’s so in love with her, and despite the relative uncertainty of what next season might look like…she has confidence they’ll make it through. “Thank you.”

    “That was all you, baby,” Kara says, soft. Lena threads forward to kiss her right as the announcer calls her name, and then Kara and the poor PA are shoving her out onto the platform ahead of them.

    -

    BALROTHERY, IRELAND

    “How can you drink that shit?” Kara asks, settled ingloriously in one of the old patio chairs in Dara and Aileen's back garden. Lena is sitting peacefully in her lap, smoking a cigar that’s been produced from…somewhere. She knows Oscar is the one who had handed one to her and the other to Sam.

    “It isn’t shit, Kara, you are simply uncultured,” Brainy returns from his own chair, swirling around his fifth gin and tonic. “Gin is beautiful. Just because you vomited it once in college doesn’t mean it is shit.”

    “I never much liked gin myself,” Aileen says, reaching forward to the little table they’ve piled chips on and throwing one in her mouth. 

    “That’s because you’re a lightweight,” Connor says. Connor is Oscar’s husband, and he’s got a beard so beautiful that Jack has been trying to get him to reveal what products he uses for almost the whole time they’ve been here. Connor keeps insisting that he uses none, and Jack keeps insisting that can’t possibly be true. 

    “Keep your mouth shut, Connie,” Aileen says, pointing at him with her drink in hand. Lena laughs along with all the rest of the assembled parties, breathing out smoke away from Kara’s head and wondering if she can convince someone to bring her another chicken burger from the pile they’ve shoved into the oven to keep warm while they party. Probably someone - she turns to look over Kara’s shoulder and spies Nia, Oscar, and Sam doing some sort of dance together quite badly. 

    “Dara!” Lena says, spotting him where he’s coming out the sliding glass door. He freezes in place, carrying four different cans of beer in one hand. 

    “What, you hellion?” he yells back.

    “Can you get me a chicken burger?” she asks. 

    “Ach, Christ, you’re as demanding as you were when you were three!” Dara says, but sets the cans of beer on the picnic table and heads back in anyway. Jack snatches one off the table immediately, wandering back toward the trio of dancers and laughing as he cracks it open.

    It’s nice. She feels…drunk. Drunk and at home.

    “Y’okay?” Kara asks, her lips brushing at Lena’s jaw. It tickles. It sends a shiver of heat up and down her spine, and Lena once again sort of regrets that after they call this night done, they have a car ride back to Dublin that they’re sharing with all of their friends. Maybe she should buy a house here. 

    “Yeah,” Lena says, turning her head to kiss Kara. “M’good.”

    -

    EPILOGUE: GEORGIA, UNITED STATES - AUGUSTA NATIONAL GOLF COURSE - MASTERS TOURNAMENT PAR 3 CONTEST

    “This thing is fucking hot. How could I forget what these are like?” Kara mutters, tugging at the collar of her white jumpsuit. Lena is leaning on her putter, watching as Cat makes her son Carter take the putt from far across the green. He’s a nice kid, a bit younger than Ruby. He and Kara did an extended handshake when they ran into each other last night at the hotel.

    “Well, you look fantastic, darling,” Lena says. Kara snorts.

    “No one has ever looked fantastic in an all-white jumpsuit, and don’t you dare lie and say I did last year,” Kara says. Carter takes a few swings, but does manage to hole his putt to a loud cheer from the audience. Kara takes her club and picks up Lena’s much-slimmed down bag and shoulders it, turning to follow the path to the next hole.

    As they go, excited talking follows them. Kara’s arrival for the day had been accompanied by a loud cheer, everyone pleased to see her after months away from the tour and Lena’s bag. Lena waves to a patch of fans in rainbow Maserati shirts, and they wave back with enthusiasm. 

    “Aside from the jumpsuit, how does the brief return to caddying feel?” Lena asks idly. Their schedules haven’t especially lined up as the season has proceeded, but Kara had made sure to work with her editors to get the week of the Masters off - she had claimed it wasn’t only because Lena had put potstickers on the Champions Dinner menu, but Lena had strong doubts. When Lena had asked offhand if Kara wanted to participate as her guest caddy in the par three contest, she’d jumped up and down and eventually tripped over the rug in their living room. Jack had taken his one day off from Lena’s bag and disappeared somewhere with Lana Lang.

    “Fantastic. Showstopping. Definitely not better than how I was in Paris last week for my story,” Kara says. Lena rolls her eyes. “Which reminds me, I have to put Ratatouille in for movie night.”

    “Kara, someone from Belgium keeps calling your phone,” Nia says, following along the inner ropes reserved for VIP guests and family. Brainy is trailing behind her with his finger in the air as though he’s testing for wind. There’s no wind, which is unfortunate, because it is horrifically humid.

    “Oh, that’s my source on this art thief ring thing,” Kara says. “I’ll call him after we finish, I have to get a translator and it’s a whole thing.”

    “Right,” Nia says, looking very confused as she puts Kara’s phone on silent.

    “Oh, did you manage to get ahold of him?” Lena asks. Kara hums and nods, smiling over at Lena. Ray-Ban has continued on sending Kara sunglasses even though she’s not been actively on the tour with Lena this season. She looks as beautiful as ever, her hair drawn back in a braid and golden in the sun of the Georgia sky. Lena has her favorite person in the world walking right next to her. After this weekend, Kara is flying back to National City and Lena’s off to South Carolina for another tournament, and then they’re taking a vacation in the Maldives.

    “Yep. Turns out he was just on vacation, not being tortured by the art thieves," Kara says. 

    “Well that’s good,” Lena says. They round the corner to the tee box then, and the great wide expanse of Augusta National lies before them, enormous trees rising on both sides of the fairway. It’s beautiful. It’s fun. It’s relaxing. “So, you know how last year you kept talking about getting me in a green jacket and lingerie?”

    “Do you think that’s something I could forget, babe?” Kara asks, laughing and setting the bag down. “I’m still bummed the editors wouldn’t go for it and it’s been months. Why would you looking sexy in lingerie detract from the seriousness of Lex and Lillian’s mistreatment of you and dozens of other golfers? It doesn’t make sense.”

    “I have a gift for you,” Lena says, interrupting what is sure to be a diatribe of Kara’s continued aggrievance with the editors of TIME on her longform article’s choice of photography. Kara looks at her for a moment, before she pulls her sunglasses straight off her face to reveal her blue blue eyes looking both wild and eager.

    “On the hood of the Maserati?” Kara asks. Lena rolls her eyes. The black metallic paint of her now-cherished and very fast car had been very hot under her bare ass. But Kara doesn’t need to know that, at least right now.

    “Eight iron,” Lena says, holding out her hand. Kara stares at her over the bag, mouth open, eyes focused. Lena is forced to snap in her face as the attendant at the tee box looks a little confused by their pause. “Kara. Club.”

    Kara scrambles, rattling the bag and jamming her sunglasses back on her face before producing a club and handing it over.

    “I think you should swing nine,” Kara says. “You’ve got a lot of energy.”

    “Thank you, darling,” Lena says, leaning forward to press a kiss to Kara’s cheek. Kara laughs, her hand sliding along Lena’s waist as she brushes by. 

    “Save some for me, though,” Kara says, just before Lena passes out of range. Lena laughs, shaking her head. She steps up to the tee box, plants her tee, places the ball, and eyes the hole placement. It’s nice and simple for the par 3 contest. When she steps up, she takes two practice swings, and then hits the ball with an easy swing. It takes flight, and Lena eyes it as it goes. Kara had been right. 

    It hits the green, takes backspin, and then comes to a rolling stop within a foot of the flag. The crowd goes wild. 

    Chapter End Notes

    Thank you for reading this fic - I know, I know, you "know nothing about golf" or "hate golf" or "can't believe I'm reading a fic about golf." But you read it! And I hope you enjoyed it.

    Golf has many, many issues, some inherent and some learned, but I actually really do think it is a good sport. If you get a chance to play, try it once or twice.

    Anyway, thank you for sticking with this journey and reading this story which is, in fact, not really about golf at all. Thank you to my wife Lynne for giving me her hand-me-down golf clubs and beta-ing this story.
